dimension defining the injection plane.\n\n    Phase 1 supports two angular specifications:\n    - ``FixedInPlaneKSpec``: constant in-plane k-vector (frequency-dependent angle)\n    - ``FixedAngleSpec``: fixed propagation angle (frequency-independent)\n\n    Example\n    -------\n    >>> from autofdtd import GaussianPulse, PlaneWave\n    >>> pulse = GaussianPulse(freq0=200e12, fwidth=20e12)\n    >>> pw = PlaneWave(\n    ...     size=(0, 0, 10),\n    ...     source_time=pulse,\n    ...     pol_angle=0.0,\n    ...     direction=\"+\",\n    ... )\n    \"\"\"\n\n    type: Literal[\"PlaneWave\"] = \"PlaneWave\"\n    center: Vec3 = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)\n    size: Vec3\n    source_time: GaussianPulse | ContinuousWave | BroadbandPulse | CustomSourceTime\n    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"] = \"+\"\n    angle_theta: float = 0.0\n    angle_phi: float = 0.0\n    pol_angle: float = 0.0\n    angular_spec: AngularSpec = Field(default_factory=FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n    name: str | None = None\n    interpolate: bool = True\n    confine_to_bounds: bool = False\n\n    @field_validator(\"center\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_center(cls, value: Sequence[object]) -> Vec3:\n        if len(value) != 3:\n            raise ValueError(\"center must contain exactly three components\")\n        normalized = (float(value[0]), float(value[1]), float(value[2]))\n        if any(not math.isfinite(component) for component in normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\"center components must be finite\")\n        return normalized\n\n    @field_validator(\"size\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_size(cls, value: Sequence[object]) -> Vec3:\n        if len(value) != 3:\n            raise ValueError(\"size must contain exactly three components\")\n        normalized = tuple(float(v) for v in value)\n        if any(not math.isfinite(component) for component in normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\"size components must be finite\")\n        # Must have exactly one zero dimension (planar source)\n        zero_count = sum(1 for s in normalized if math.isclose(s, 0.0))\n        if zero_count != 1:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"PlaneWave requires exactly one zero-size dimension \"\n                f\"(planar source), got size={normalized}\"\n            )\n        return normalized\n\n    @field_validator(\"name\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_name(cls, value: str | None) -> str | None:\n        return _normalize_name(value)\n\n    @field_validator(\"source_time\", mode=\"before\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_source_time(cls, value: object) -> SourceTimeModel:\n        return source_time_model_from_value(value)\n\n    @field_validator(\"angle_theta\", \"angle_phi\", \"pol_angle\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_angle(cls, value: float) -> float:\n        normalized = float(value)\n        if not math.isfinite(normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\"angle components must be finite\")\n        return normalized\n\n    @property\n    def injection_axis(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Axis of source normal (the zero-size dimension, injection direction).\"\"\"\n        for i, extent in enumerate(self.size):\n            if math.isclose(extent, 0.0):\n                return i\n        raise ValueError(\"PlaneWave must have exactly one zero-size dimension\")\n\n    @property\n    def _tangential_axes(self) -> tuple[int, int]:\n        \"\"\"The two tangential axes for this planar source.\"\"\"\n        axis = self.injection_axis\n        return tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != axis)\n\n    @property\n    def placement_kind(self) -> Literal[\"sheet\"]:\n        \"\"\"Always a sheet (planar) placement.\"\"\"\n        return \"sheet\"\n\n    @property\n    def support_bounds(self) -> tuple[Vec3, Vec3]:\n        half = tuple(extent / 2.0 for extent in self.size)\n        lower = tuple(c - h for c, h in zip(self.center, half, strict=True))\n        upper = tuple(c + h for c, h in zip(self.center, half, strict=True))\n        return lower, upper\n\n    @property\n    def _dir_vector(self) -> Vec3:\n        \"\"\"Source direction normal vector in cartesian coordinates.\"\"\"\n\n        # Propagation vector assuming injection along z\n        radius = 1.0 if self.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n        dx = radius * math.cos(self.angle_phi) * math.sin(self.angle_theta)\n        dy = radius * math.sin(self.angle_phi) * math.sin(self.angle_theta)\n        dz = radius * math.cos(self.angle_theta)\n\n        # Move to original injection axis\n        return self._unpop_axis(dz, (dx, dy))\n\n    @property\n    def _pol_vector(self) -> Vec3:\n        \"\"\"Source polarization normal vector in cartesian coordinates.\"\"\"\n\n        # Polarization vector assuming propagation along z\n        pol_x = math.cos(self.pol_angle)\n        pol_y = math.sin(self.pol_angle)\n        pol_z = 0.0\n\n        # Rotate polarization back to original propagation axes\n        # First rotate around y-axis by angle_theta\n        cos_t = math.cos(self.angle_theta)\n        sin_t = math.sin(self.angle_theta)\n        pol_x_rot = pol_x * cos_t + pol_z * sin_t\n        pol_y_rot = pol_y\n        pol_z_rot = -pol_x * sin_t + pol_z * cos_t\n\n        # Then rotate around z-axis by angle_phi\n        cos_p = math.cos(self.angle_phi)\n        sin_p = math.sin(self.angle_phi)\n        pol_x_final = pol_x_rot * cos_p - pol_y_rot * sin_p\n        pol_y_final = pol_x_rot * sin_p + pol_y_rot * cos_p\n        pol_z_final = pol_z_rot\n\n        # Move to original injection axis\n        return self._unpop_axis(pol_z_final, (pol_x_final, pol_y_final))\n\n    def _unpop_axis(self, z_component: float, xy_pair: tuple[float, float]) -> Vec3:\n        \"\"\"Restore components to the original injection axis ordering.\"\"\"\n        axis = self.injection_axis\n        result = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]\n        if axis == 0:\n            result[0] = z_component\n            result[1] = xy_pair[0]\n            result[2] = xy_pair[1]\n        elif axis == 1:\n            result[0] = xy_pair[0]\n            result[1] = z_component\n            result[2] = xy_pair[1]\n        else:  # axis == 2\n            result[0] = xy_pair[0]\n            result[1] = xy_pair[1]\n            result[2] = z_component\n        return tuple(result)\n\n    @property\n    def is_fixed_angle(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Whether the plane wave uses fixed-angle specification (frequency-independent).\"\"\"\n        return isinstance(self.angular_spec, FixedAngleSpec) and self.angle_theta != 0.0\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"AbstractAngularSpec\",\n    \"FixedInPlaneKSpec\",\n    \"FixedAngleSpec\",\n    \"AngularSpec\",\n    \"angular_spec_model_from_value\",\n    \"PlaneWave\",\n]","numLines":259,"startLine":1,"totalLines":259}}}

0)\n149\t    if position <= values[0]:\n150\t        return (0, 0)\n151\t    if position >= values[-1]:\n152\t        last = values.size - 1\n153\t        return (last, last)\n154\t    upper =    axis: ResolvedGridAxis,\n161\t    *,\n162\t    center: float,\n163\t    size: float,\n164\t    interpolate: bool,\n165\t    confine_to_bounds: bool,\n166\t) -> CompiledAxisPlacement:\n167\t    if centers=(float(centers[lower]),),\n201\t            )\n202\t        left_center = centers[lower]\n203\t        right_center = centers[upper]\n204\t        fraction = float((center - left_center) / complex(self.source.source_time.amp_time(time)[0])\n751\t\n752\t\n753\tdef _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n754\t    angle_theta: float,\n755\t    angle_phi: float,\n756\t    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"],\n757\t    injection_axis: int,\n758\t    n_medium: float = 1.0,\n759\t) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n760\t    \"\"\"Compute k-vector components from angles for a plane wave.\n761\t\n762\t    Args:\n763\t        angle_theta: Polar angle from injection axis\n764\t        angle_phi: Azimuth angle in plane perpendicular to injection axis\n765\t        direction: Propagation direction along injection axis (+ or -)\n766\t        injection_axis: Which axis is the injection normal (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n767\t        n_medium: Refractive index of the background medium\n768\t\n769\t    Returns:\n770\t        kx, ky, kz components of the wavevector\n771\t    \"\"\"\n772\t    c0 = 2.998e8  # speed of light in vacuum\n773\t\n774\t    # Direction multiplier\n775\t    dir_mult = 1.0 if direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n776\t\n777\t    # For a plane wave propagating at angles theta, phi relative to injection axis:\n778\t    # kx = k * sin(theta) * cos(phi)\n779\t    # ky = k * sin(theta) * sin(phi)\n780\t    # kz = k * cos(theta) for injection along z\n781\t    # Then we rotate to the actual injection axis\n782\t\n783\t    sin_theta = math.sin(angle_theta)\n784\t    cos_theta = math.cos(angle_theta)\n785\t    sin_phi = math.sin(angle_phi)\n786\t    cos_phi = math.cos(angle_phi)\n787\t\n788\t    # k magnitude in the medium\n789\t    k_mag = dir_mult * n_medium * 2 * math.pi / c0\n790\t\n791\t    # Temporary k vector assuming injection along z\n792\t    kx_t = k_mag * sin_theta * cos_phi\n793\t    ky_t = k_mag * sin_theta * sin_phi\n794\t    kz_t = k_mag * cos_theta\n795\t\n796\t    # Rotate to actual injection axis\n797\t    if injection_axis == 0:\n798\t        # Injection along x: kx=z, ky=y, kz=x -> we need kz=kx_t, kx=ky_t, ky=kz_t\n799\t        return (kz_t, ky_t, kx_t)\n800\t    elif injection_axis == 1:\n801\t        # Injection along y: kx=x, ky=z, kz=y -> we need kx=kx_t, ky=kz_t, kz=ky_t\n802\t        return (kx_t, kz_t, ky_t)\n803\t    else:\n804\t        # Injection along z: kx=x, ky=y, kz=z\n805\t        return (kx_t, ky_t, kz_t)\n806\t\n807\t\n808\tdef compile_plane_wave(\n809\t    source: PlaneWave,\n810\t    *,\n811\t    grid: strict=True,\n839\t    ):\n840\t        for y_index, y_weight in zip(\n841\t            axis_placements[1].indices,\n842\t            axis_placements[1].weights,\n843\t            strict=True,\n844\t )\n857\t\n858\t    # The k-vector functions will be evaluated at runtime per frequency\n859\t    # For now, store the parameters for later evaluation\n860\t    def _make_k_funcs(inj_axis: int, direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"]):\n861\t        \"\"\"Create k-vector functions for the plane wave at a given frequency.\"\"\"\n862\t\n863\t        def kx_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> math.pi * freq) if freq > 0 else 0.0\n868\t\n869\t        def ky_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n870\t            _, ky, _ = _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n871\t complex(self.source.source_time.amp_time(time)[0])\n\n\ndef _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n    angle_theta: float,\n    angle_phi: float,\n    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"],\n    injection_axis: int,\n    n_medium: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    \"\"\"Compute k-vector components from angles for a plane wave.\n\n    Args:\n        angle_theta: Polar angle from injection axis\n        angle_phi: Azimuth angle in plane perpendicular to injection axis\n        direction: Propagation direction along injection axis (+ or -)\n        injection_axis: Which axis is the injection normal (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        n_medium: Refractive index of the background medium\n\n    Returns:\n        kx, ky, kz components of the wavevector\n    \"\"\"\n    c0 = 2.998e8  # speed of light in vacuum\n\n    # Direction multiplier\n    dir_mult = 1.0 if direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n\n    # For a plane wave propagating at angles theta, phi relative to injection axis:\n    # kx = k * sin(theta) * cos(phi)\n    # ky = k * sin(theta) * sin(phi)\n    # kz = k * cos(theta) for injection along z\n    # Then we rotate to the actual injection axis\n\n    sin_theta = math.sin(angle_theta)\n    cos_theta = math.cos(angle_theta)\n    sin_phi = math.sin(angle_phi)\n    cos_phi = math.cos(angle_phi)\n\n    # k magnitude in the medium\n    k_mag = dir_mult * n_medium * 2 * math.pi / c0\n\n    # Temporary k vector assuming injection along z\n    kx_t = k_mag * sin_theta * cos_phi\n    ky_t = k_mag * sin_theta * sin_phi\n    kz_t = k_mag * cos_theta\n\n    # Rotate to actual injection axis\n    if injection_axis == 0:\n        # Injection along x: kx=z, ky=y, kz=x -> we need kz=kx_t, kx=ky_t, ky=kz_t\n        return (kz_t, ky_t, kx_t)\n    elif injection_axis == 1:\n        # Injection along y: kx=x, ky=z, kz=y -> we need kx=kx_t, ky=kz_t, kz=ky_t\n        return (kx_t, kz_t, ky_t)\n    else:\n        # Injection along z: kx=x, ky=y, kz=z\n        return (kx_t, ky_t, kz_t)\n\n\ndef compile_plane_wave(\n    source: PlaneWave,\n    *,\n    grid: ResolvedGrid,\n) -> CompiledPlaneWave:\n    \"\"\"Compile a plane wave source onto the resolved primal-cell strict=True,\n    ):\n        for y_index, y_weight in zip(\n            axis_placements[1].indices,\n            axis_placements[1].weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for z_index, z_weight in zip(\n                axis_placements[2].indices,\n                axis_placements[2].weights,\n                strict=True,\n            ):\n angular_spec_type: Literal[\"FixedInPlaneK\", \"FixedAngle\"] = (\n        \"FixedAngle\" if source.is_fixed_angle else \"FixedInPlaneK\"\n    )\n\n    # The k-vector functions will be evaluated at the plane wave at a given frequency.\"\"\"\n\n        def kx_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n            kx, _, _ = _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n                source.angle_theta, source.angle_phi, direction, inj_axis, n\n            )\n            return kx / (2 * math.pi * freq) if freq > 0 else 0.0\n\n        def ky_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n            _, axis_placements: tuple[CompiledAxisPlacement, CompiledAxisPlacement, CompiledAxisPlacement],\n    injection_axis: int,\n    waist_radius: float,\n    waist_distance: float,\n) -> tuple[float, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Compute Gaussian envelope weights for beam cross-section.\n\n    Args:\n        axis_placements: The compiled axis placements for x, y, z\n        injection_axis: The injection axis (0=x, 1=y, CompiledAxisPlacement, CompiledAxisPlacement],\n    injection_axis: int,\n    waist_radius_x: float,\n    waist_radius_y: float,\n    waist_distance_x: float,\n    waist_distance_y: float,\n) -> tuple[float, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Compute astigmatic Gaussian envelope weights for beam cross-section.\n\n    Args:\n        axis_placements: The compiled axis placements for x, y, z\n        injection_axis: The injection axis (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        waist_radius_x: Beam waist radius in x direction\n        waist_radius_y: Beam waist radius in y direction\n        waist_distance_x: axis_placements[tang_axes[0]].centers,\n        axis_placements[tang_axes[1]].centers,\n    ]\n\n    weights: list[float] = []\n\n    # For beam injection, we compute the astigmatic Gaussian \"\"\"Apply a compiled mode source to the supplied field buffers.\n296\t\n297\t    ModeSource uses the equivalence principle: tangential E and H field\n298\t    components from the mode profile are converted to J and M currents.\n299\t    For direction=\"+\", H fields contribute to E injection and E fields\n300\t    contribute to H injection (forward propagation). Get the tangential axes\n337\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n338\t    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n339\t\n340\t    # Normalize mode fields for injection (power normalization)\n341\t    power_norm = 1.0 / math.sqrt(compiled_source.mode_power) if compiled_source.mode_power > 0 else 1.0\n342\t\n343\t    for placement, weight in zip(\n344\t        compiled_source.placements,\n345\t        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n346\t        strict=True,\n347\t    ):\n348\t        # Get the local grid position\n349\t        idx_x, idx_y = placement[tang_axis_a], placement[tang_axis_b]\n350\t\n351\t        # Get mode field values at this position (interpolated from mode solver grid)\n352\t        # The mode fields are defined on the cross-sectional grid and are complex\n353\t        ex_val = e_data[\"Ex\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ex\" in e_data else 0.0\n354\t        ey_val = e_data[\"Ey\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ey\" in e_data else 0.0\n355\t        hx_val = h_data[\"Hx\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hx\" in h_data else 0.0\n356\t        hy_val = h_data[\"Hy\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hy\" in h_data else 0.0\n357\t\n358\t        # Apply equivalence principle for mode injection\n359\t        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E, tangential E contributes to H\n360\t        # M = -n × E (electric fields contribute to magnetic current)\n361\t        # J = n × H (magnetic fields contribute to electric current)\n362\t\n363\t        # For a planar source in xy-plane (z-injection):\n364\t        # Jx = n_y * Hz - n_z * Hy = -Hy (for nz=1, ny=0)\n365\t        # Jy = n_z * Hx - n_x * Hz = Hx (for nz=1, nx=0)\n366\t        # Mx = -n_y * Ez + n_z * Ey = Ey (for nz=1, ny=0)\n367\t        # My = -n_z * Ex + n_x * Ez = -Ex (for nz=1, nx=0)\n368\t\n369\t        if direction_sign > 0:\n370\t            # Forward propagation: H fields inject into E, E fields inject into H\n371\t            # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n372\t            # My = -Ex, Mx = Ey for z-injection\n373\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hy_val)\n374\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hx_val\n375\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ey_val\n376\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ex_val)\n377\t        else:\n378\t            # Backward propagation: E fields inject into H, H fields inject into E\n379\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hy_val\n380\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hx_val)\n381\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ey_val)\n382\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ex_val\n383\t\n384\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n385\t\n386\t\n387\tdef inject_plane_wave(\n388\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n389\t    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n390\t    compiled_source: CompiledPlaneWave,\n391\t    *,\n392\t    time: float,\n393\t    dt: float = 1.0,\n394\t    freq: float | None = None,\n395\t) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n396\t    \"\"\"Apply a compiled plane wave source to the supplied field buffers.\n397\t\n398\t    A PlaneWave injects a spatially uniform electromagnetic wave with a defined\n399\t    propagation direction and polarization. The source uses the equivalence\n400\t    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E, where n is the source normal.\n401\t\n402\t    For a plane wave, the fields are uniform across the planar injection surface\n403\t    and determined by the k-vector and polarization.\n404\t\n405\t    Args:\n406\t        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n407\t        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n408\t        compiled_source: The compiled plane wave source\n409\t        time: Current simulation time\n410\t        dt: Timestep size\n411\t        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n412\t\n413\t    Returns:\n414\t        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n415\t    \"\"\"\n416\t    if dt <= 0.0:\n417\t        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n418\t\n419\t    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n420\t    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n421\t    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n422\t        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n423\t    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n424\t        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n425\t\n426\t    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n427\t    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n428\t\n429\t    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n430\t    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n431\t    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n432\t\n433\t    # Get tangential axes\n434\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n435\t    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n436\t\n437\t    # For a plane wave, the tangential field components are uniform across\n438\t    # the source plane. We compute the E and H fields based on the\n439\t    # polarization and direction vectors.\n440\t\n441\t    # The plane wave fields in the source coordinate system are:\n442\t    # E = E0 * pol_vector * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n443\t    # H = H0 * (k_hat × pol_vector) * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n444\t    #\n445\t    # For injection via equivalence principle:\n446\t    # - The tangential H fields contribute to E via J = n × H\n447\t    # - The tangential E fields contribute to H via M = -n × E\n448\t    #\n449\t    # Since the plane wave is spatially uniform on the injection plane,\n450\t    # we evaluate at a reference point (center of the source plane).\n451\t\n452\t    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n453\t    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n454\t\n455\t    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n456\t    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n457\t    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n458\t\n459\t    # Normalize direction\n460\t    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n461\t    if dir_mag > 0:\n462\t        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n463\t    else:\n464\t        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n465\t\n466\t    # Polarization direction\n467\t    pol_hat = pol_vec\n468\t\n469\t    # For plane wave injection on a planar surface, we inject at all\n470\t    # placements with uniform weight. The plane wave fields are computed\n471\t    # based on the k-vector direction and polarization.\n472\t\n473\t    # The tangential components of E and H that contribute to injection\n474\t    # are determined by the cross products with the injection normal.\n475\t\n476\t    # For n = (0, 0, 1) (z-injection):\n477\t    # J = n × H = (Hx, Hy, 0) → contributes to (Ez,)\n478\t    # M = -n × E = (-Ex, -Ey, 0) → contributes to (Hz,)\n479\t    #\n480\t    # More generally, we need to compute the tangential components of\n481\t    # E and H that are perpendicular to the injection direction.\n482\t\n483\t    # The E field at the source plane is along the polarization direction\n484\t    # The H field is along k_hat × pol_hat (perpendicular to both)\n485\t\n486\t    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n487\t    # H_dir = k_hat × pol_hat\n488\t    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n489\t    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n490\t    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n491\t    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n492\t\n493\t    # The magnitude ratio between E and H in a plane wave is the impedance\n494\t    # For vacuum: Z0 = sqrt(μ0/ε0) ≈ 377 ohms\n495\t    # In terms of normalized units used in FDTD: E/H = Z0\n496\t    z0_normalized = 1.0  # In our normalized units where c=1\n497\t\n498\t    # For a uniform plane wave, the field amplitude is uniform across the plane.\n499\t    # The injection injects the equivalent currents at each cell.\n500\t\n501\t    # For each placement, inject the plane wave fields via equivalence principle\n502\t    for placement, weight in zip(\n503\t        compiled_source.placements,\n504\t        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n505\t        strict=True,\n506\t    ):\n507\t        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n508\t        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n509\t        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n510\t\n511\t        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n512\t        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n513\t        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n514\t\n515\t        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n516\t        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n517\t        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n518\t\n519\t        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E\n520\t        # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n521\t        # For general injection axis:\n522\t        # The current density J = n × H gives components along tang_axes\n523\t\n524\t        if direction_sign > 0:\n525\t            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n526\t            # E is updated along injection axis from H tangential\n527\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n528\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n529\t            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n530\t            # H is updated along injection axis from E tangential\n531\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n532\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n533\t        else:\n534\t            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n535\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n536\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n537\t            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n538\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n539\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n540\t\n541\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n542\t\n543\t\n544\tdef inject_gaussian_beam(\n545\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n546\t    magnetic_field: shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    target = updated_electric if compiled_source.field_kind == \"electric\" else updated_magnetic\n    axis = compiled_source.component_axis\n\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        target[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_point_dipole(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledPointDipole,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        target[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_custom_current_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledCustomCurrentSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled custom current source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    CustomCurrentSource can inject both electric and magnetic field components\n    based on the provided field data arrays.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Apply electric field components\n    if compiled_source.has_electric and compiled_source.e_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.e_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Ex\", \"Ey\", \"Ez\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Apply field data - simplified version using index mapping\n updated_electric[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight * field_values[idx]\n\n    # Apply magnetic field components\n    if compiled_source.has_magnetic and compiled_source.h_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.h_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Hx\", \"Hy\", \"Hz\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        updated_magnetic[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight * field_values[idx]\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_custom_field_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledCustomFieldSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled custom field source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    CustomFieldSource uses the equivalence principle on a planar surface.\n    For tangential field components provided:\n    - Electric field components (Ex, Ey on xy-plane) contribute via M = -n × E\n    - Magnetic field components (Hx, Hy on xy-plane) contribute via J = n × H\n\n    The direction sign determines whether the source injects forward (+)\n    or backward (-) relative to the injection axis normal.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward, -1 for backward\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n\n    # Injection axis determines the normal direction for equivalence principle\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Apply electric field components via equivalence principle: M = -n × E\n    # For a planar source, tangential E fields contribute to H update\n    if compiled_source.has_electric and compiled_source.e_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.e_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Ex\", \"Ey\", \"Ez\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Skip the injection axis component (normal component is not used in equivalence)\n                    if axis == injection_axis:\n                        continue\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        # Electric fields contribute to magnetic field update via M = -n × E\n                        # This injects into H along the injection axis\n                        updated_magnetic[placement][injection_axis] += (\n                            amplitude * weight * direction_sign * field_values[idx]\n                        )\n\n    # Apply magnetic field components via equivalence principle: J = n × H\n    # For a planar source, tangential H fields contribute to E update\n    if compiled_source.has_magnetic and compiled_source.h_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.h_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Hx\", \"Hy\", \"Hz\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Skip the injection axis component (normal component is not used in equivalence)\n                    if axis == injection_axis:\n                        continue\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        # Magnetic fields contribute to electric field update via J = n × H\n                        # This injects into E along the injection axis\n                        updated_electric[placement][injection_axis] += (\n                            amplitude * weight * direction_sign * field_values[idx]\n                        )\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_mode_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledModeSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled mode source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    ModeSource uses the equivalence principle: tangential E and H field\n    components from the mode profile are converted to J and M currents.\n    For direction=\"+\", H fields contribute to E injection and E fields\n    contribute to H injection (forward propagation). For direction=\"-\",\n    the reverse holds (backward propagation).\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled mode source with field data\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    e_data = compiled_source.e_field_data\n    h_data = compiled_source.h_field_data\n    x_coords = compiled_source.x_coords\n    y_coords = compiled_source.y_coords\n\n    # Get the tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Normalize mode fields for injection (power normalization)\n    power_norm = 1.0 / math.sqrt(compiled_source.mode_power) if compiled_source.mode_power > 0 else 1.0\n\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Get the local grid position\n        idx_x, idx_y = placement[tang_axis_a], placement[tang_axis_b]\n\n        # Get mode field values at this position (interpolated from mode solver grid)\n        # The mode fields are defined on the cross-sectional grid and are complex\n        ex_val = e_data[\"Ex\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ex\" in e_data else 0.0\n        ey_val = e_data[\"Ey\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ey\" in e_data else 0.0\n        hx_val = h_data[\"Hx\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hx\" in h_data else 0.0\n        hy_val = h_data[\"Hy\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hy\" in h_data else 0.0\n\n        # Apply equivalence principle for mode injection\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E, tangential E contributes to H\n        # M = -n × E (electric fields contribute to magnetic current)\n        # J = n × H (magnetic fields contribute to electric current)\n\n        # For a planar source in xy-plane (z-injection):\n        # Jx = n_y * Hz - n_z * Hy = -Hy (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # Jy = n_z * Hx - n_x * Hz = Hx (for nz=1, nx=0)\n        # Mx = -n_y * Ez + n_z * Ey = Ey (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # My = -n_z * Ex + n_x * Ez = -Ex (for nz=1, nx=0)\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward propagation: H fields inject into E, E fields inject into H\n            # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n            # My = -Ex, Mx = Ey for z-injection\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hy_val)\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hx_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ey_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ex_val)\n        else:\n            # Backward propagation: E fields inject into H, H fields inject into E\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hy_val\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hx_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ey_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ex_val\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_plane_wave(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledPlaneWave,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled plane wave source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A PlaneWave injects a spatially uniform electromagnetic wave with a defined\n    propagation direction and polarization. The source uses the equivalence\n    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E, where n is the source normal.\n\n    For a plane wave, the fields are uniform across the planar injection surface\n    and determined by the k-vector and polarization.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled plane wave source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # For a plane wave, the tangential field components are uniform across\n    # the source plane. We compute the E and H fields based on the\n    # polarization and direction vectors.\n\n    # The plane wave fields in the source coordinate system are:\n    # E = E0 * pol_vector * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    # H = H0 * (k_hat × pol_vector) * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    #\n    # For injection via equivalence principle:\n    # - The tangential H fields contribute to E via J = n × H\n    # - The tangential E fields contribute to H via M = -n × E\n    #\n    # Since the plane wave is spatially uniform on the injection plane,\n    # we evaluate at a reference point (center of the source plane).\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # For plane wave injection on a planar surface, we inject at all\n    # placements with uniform weight. The plane wave fields are computed\n    # based on the k-vector direction and polarization.\n\n    # The tangential components of E and H that contribute to injection\n    # are determined by the cross products with the injection normal.\n\n    # For n = (0, 0, 1) (z-injection):\n    # J = n × H = (Hx, Hy, 0) → contributes to (Ez,)\n    # M = -n × E = (-Ex, -Ey, 0) → contributes to (Hz,)\n    #\n    # More generally, we need to compute the tangential components of\n    # E and H that are perpendicular to the injection direction.\n\n    # The E field at the source plane is along the polarization direction\n    # The H field is along k_hat × pol_hat (perpendicular to both)\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    # H_dir = k_hat × pol_hat\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # The magnitude ratio between E and H in a plane wave is the impedance\n    # For vacuum: Z0 = sqrt(μ0/ε0) ≈ 377 ohms\n    # In terms of normalized units used in FDTD: E/H = Z0\n    z0_normalized = 1.0  # In our normalized units where c=1\n\n    # For a uniform plane wave, the field amplitude is uniform across the plane.\n    # The injection injects the equivalent currents at each cell.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the plane wave fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E\n        # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n        # For general injection axis:\n        # The current density J = n × H gives components along tang_axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            # E is updated along injection axis from H tangential\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            # H is updated along injection axis from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A GaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave with\n    a defined propagation direction, polarization, and waist parameters.\n    The source uses the equivalence principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n\n    The Gaussian envelope determines the amplitude profile across the beam\n    cross-section, with peak amplitude at the center and falling off radially.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled Gaussian beam source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For a Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies across the beam cross-section\n    # according to the Gaussian envelope. The beam_weights already include this\n    # spatial variation.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the Gaussian beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Combined weight: placement_weight (grid interpolation) * beam_weight (Gaussian envelope)\n        combined_weight = placement_weight * beam_weight\n\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    An AstigmaticGaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave\n    with separate waist radii in x and y directions. The source uses the equivalence\n    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For an astigmatic Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies with\n    # separate x and y envelope parameters.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Combined weight: placement_weight (grid interpolation) * beam_weight (Gaussian envelope)\n        combined_weight = placement_weight * beam_weight\n\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n","numLines":778,"startLine":1,"totalLines":778}}}

Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n81\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n82\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n83\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n84\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n85\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n86\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n87\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n88\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n89\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n90\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n91\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n92\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n93\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n94\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n95\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n96\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n97\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n98\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n99\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n100\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n101\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n102\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n103\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n104\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n105\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n106\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n107\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n108\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n109\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n110\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n111\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n112\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n113\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n114\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n115\t\n116\t## compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n131\t- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n133\t- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget contract.\n138\t\n139\t## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n140\t\n141\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n142\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n143\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n144\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n145\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n146\t- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n147\t- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n148\t- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n149\t\n150\t## Task Handoffs (continued)\n151\t\n152\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e7cefda8-7480-4bc3-bf11-7dc31cf60538","uuid":"bb9fba6a-f044-4a8c-820a-1874e4ead136","timestamp":"2026-04-07T04:21:35.314Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/workspace/autofdtd/phase1/working-memory.md","content":"# Phase 1 Maxwell Solver Loop Working Memory\n\nUse this file for cumulative findings, reusable facts, decisions, and cross-task notes.\n\nKeep it concise.
